- **The four data layers are the standard, and the template's figures moved into `src/logic/`.** Thirty-eight modules that computed a figure or wrote a sentence — thirty-six under `src/views/`, the status list's `summarise`, and the alerts the dashboard layout reads — live in `src/logic/` now, and every import names the path each has. `docs/data-layers.md` is the standard: the four layers between a database and the screen, the contract a backend answers, the seven direct reads, and the order a product with its own API or tables follows.
  - **What a team does.** Run `update`; it reports `migration:four-layers` for each of your own files that still imports a module from a path the move left, and its instruction names all thirty-eight with the path each left. Rewrite each of those imports in your own `src/views/` producers and pages to `src/logic/`, or move the module itself beside the work it does. Two symbols left the cards they were exported from and must move with them: `afterStart` is `@/logic/after-start`, and `summarise` is `@/logic/summarise`.
  - **Doing nothing.** A project that imported neither symbol keeps building: the move is in this template, not in your project, so every path your own files name is still there. A file of yours that imported `afterStart` from `src/components/patterns/incident-card`, or `summarise` from `src/components/patterns/status-list`, stops compiling — both cards are Meridian's and the update replaces them. What no project gets by doing nothing is the layer: your figures stay where they were, and the six rules below stay silent about them until you move them and say so.
  - **Three helpers arrive managed.** `src/logic/after-start.ts`, `src/logic/derivations.ts` and `src/logic/summarise.ts` are Meridian's from this release, because a shipped card imports them: `update` replaces each when you have not edited it, and stages base, ours and new when you have. Every other module under `src/logic/` is yours.
  - **Six rules, off until you ask.** `node scripts/check.ts` checks `data-imports-neither`, `logic-does-not-import-views`, `views-produce-nothing`, `presentation-does-not-read`, `no-direct-query` and `client-does-not-import-a-reading-module`. Every one is silent until your own `scripts/check.local.ts` exports `fourLayers = true`; until then the check runs exactly as it did before. `docs/data-layers.md`'s "The six rules, and the switch" says what each asks and what skipping it costs.

### Fixed

- **The first load carried a second copy of the shell.** Turbopack's chunker merges small chunks and duplicates what it cannot merge: after the data layer stopped importing `@/views`, every route's first load held a second copy of the shell and the shared UI set — 40.3 and 29.9 KiB of it on `/engineering` — and three routes went over budget, `/engineering` 832 KiB against the 820 KiB cap, `/business` at the cap, and `/customers/[id]` 5.6% over its baseline. Smaller chunks are reusable across pages, so `experimental.turbopackChunking` now asks the chunker for them (`minChunkSize: 35000`, `maxChunkCountPerGroup: 80`): `/engineering` 811 KiB, and a page asks for 27 chunks where it asked for 23. A chunk is also a script tag in the page's HTML, so the split is taken as coarse as the HTML budget allows — every page stays within 3.8% of its baseline HTML, `/keys` the closest — and no cap, baseline or page was moved.

- **A created project's `pnpm verify` no longer fails on this repository's own record.** Two checks that ship with the template read files only this checkout has — the changelog beside the CLI's migrations and their tests, and the five guides the skill is delivered from, which `create` installs at `.claude/skills/` and `.agents/skills/` instead. Each now asks only where its subject exists, which is a checkout carrying `cli/src/migrations.ts`: a created or adopted project has no such record to check, and in this repository both run exactly as they did.

## [0.27.1] · 2026-10-24

### Fixed

- **`verify` and `live` start the app they measure on loopback** ([#81](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/81)). `next start` with no host bound every interface, so for the half hour a full run takes, the app under test — served with the fake data source — answered on the local network. Both start it with `-H 127.0.0.1`; every client of a run already reached it there.
- **The keyboard walk and the presses open a disclosure before reaching into it** ([#82](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/82)). A closed `<details>` lays its content out without drawing it, so a button inside was tagged as visible and never reached, and its press found nothing at its centre; `verify --full` failed a page with a folded table. Both suites open the disclosures they walk into, and the presses leave shut the one whose own `summary` holds the control.
- **The shell's Search button keeps its name below 40rem** ([#83](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/83)). The only word naming it was `display: none`, leaving an icon-only button with no accessible name on every view at 390px. It is `sr-only` now: the word is not drawn, and it is still the button's name.
- **The rail's menu trigger separates the product name from the workspace** ([#84](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/84)). Its visible text ran the two together, and WCAG 2.5.3 asks for the visible label inside the accessible name, where a comma alone kept them apart. A space now sits between them: invisible between two block spans, present for the name.
- **A table written in a server component lays out like one written on the client** ([#85](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/85)). `Table` found its columns by component identity, and a server component reaches the client table as lazy references, so it found none and drew every column as one. It counts them from the table's shape now: the first row of the first section that has one, its component cells, through fragments.
- **The audit fails a strip that scrolls sideways at a phone width, and text drawn past a box that does not clip it** ([#86](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/86)). A strip of chips wider than 390px hid its last items with nothing saying so, and a figure too long for its grid column passed while drawn over its neighbour and past its card. The first is reported unless it is the page's scroll region or draws the system's cut-edge fade (`scroll-fade-x`); the second is measured on the glyphs with a `Range`. The stricter reading found the Atlas counts row overflowing its cells at 1024, which now keeps four columns until `xl` and draws a rule only at the start of a row. The prose tables and code blocks that legitimately scroll now draw that fade.

## [0.27.0] · 2026-10-23

### Breaking

- **A chart's scale holds every value it draws, zero among them** ([#80](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/80)). `ticksBetween` counts a floor under zero down to a step under it, where it used to clamp it to zero and draw every value below zero under the axis and out of the card.
  - `TrendChart` without a `floor` now holds zero and every value: a series that goes under zero takes the axis down with it. An `area` is filled to the zero line from either side, not down to the lowest level.
  - `TrendChart`'s top is the data's own. It was at least 1, so a ratio that peaks at 0.4% was drawn along the floor of an axis to 100%. A chart whose values are all under 1 now draws to a step over its highest value.
  - Levels are rounded to a digit under their step, not to six places, so a ratio a millionth apart keeps one level per tick.
- **`ColumnChart` draws a value under zero as a fall** ([#78](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/78)). In the plain form, a value under zero used to draw nothing. Now the scale goes under zero, zero is drawn as the axis, and the column hangs from the zero line, rounded at its lower end, with its value under it. While no value is under zero the chart is drawn as before. Stacked, a part under zero is still no contribution: parts make a whole.

### Added

- **`TrendChart`'s `format` may be a function, with an `axisFormat`** ([#78](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/78)). From a client component, pass `(n: number) => string` for a figure no named format says exactly: a price to four decimals, a ratio to six, or an amount in a currency of its own. It reads the readout and the table, and the axis unless `axisFormat` says otherwise. A server component still passes a name, because a function cannot cross into a client component.
- The Trend chart's Atlas page draws "Below zero" and "A format of its own". The Column chart's page draws "Rises and falls".

### Fixed

- **The rail keeps the whole product name and workspace in a `title`** ([#79](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/79)). A name too long for the rail, such as 23 characters in three words, was cut with an ellipsis and nothing held the whole of it, so `scripts/audit.ts` failed every route at every width.
- **The Now embed loads its body only when it draws it.** The inline view a chat shows first draws the open incident and the services; Now's whole body, with its charts, tables and feed, is the fullscreen view, now a chunk of its own (`next/dynamic`). In a created project, `/embed/now`'s first-load JS falls from 820 KiB, at the cap, to 712 KiB. `app/embed/now/view.tsx` is a sample file the team owns, so `update` leaves a project's own copy as it is: one whose embed nears the cap can take the release's.

## [0.26.0] · 2026-10-22

### Breaking

- **A table puts its spare width in the gaps between its columns, an equal share in each.** `Table` is laid out as a CSS grid now, not by the table algorithm. Every column is as wide as its content. `justify-content: space-between` spreads the spare across the gaps, so every gap is the same whatever each column's alignment. In 0.25 the table algorithm put a column's spare inside the column, after a left-aligned value and before a right-aligned one. So a gap between a text column and a number held both columns' shares, and the gap after the number held none. Measured from the ink of one column to the ink of the next at 1920px, the gaps on /customers, /members and /tests now vary by 0 to 3% (a coefficient of variation of 0.00 to 0.03), against 42 to 53% in 0.25.1.
  - **Rows are subgrids** of the table's columns, so a row's border, its hover and its selection run across the gaps. The header row carries the sunk background and the rules.
  - **The table counts its columns** from the cells of its first row as its own JSX writes them: the head's `TableHeader` cells when it has a head, otherwise the first row's `TableCell` cells. It counts one track per column at each `hideBelow` step. Write that row's cells in the table's own JSX, not inside a component of your own, or the table cannot count them.
  - **Cells are blocks in a grid.** A cell no longer sets the row height or centres itself: the row is at least `--row-height` tall and centres its cells. A cell you styled as a table cell (`align-middle`, a fixed height, a `colSpan`) is now laid out by its row; drop those classes.
  - **The `grow` floor is 160px at every width.** It was 256px from a 672px table. A floor is also a width the column claims when its names are shorter, so the gap after the lead was half as wide again as every other gap.
  - **A text column after a number no longer takes extra left padding** (`pl-8`). With equal gaps it made one gap wider than the rest.
  - **`DataTable`'s selection checkbox sits over the lead column's left padding**, not in a column of its own, so no gap stands between a checkbox and the name it selects. It is still its own cell, first in the row, marked `data-check`. The table does not count it.
  - **`update` lists `migration:table-grid`** for a project whose own files expect the old floor (`@min-[672px]:min-w-64`, as `tests/table-cut-cell.test.tsx` did in a project created before 0.26.0) or give a `TableCell` a `colSpan`, which a grid row draws in one track. Bring the release's `tests/table-cut-cell.test.tsx` over, and draw a row that spans the table, such as an empty state, outside it or as a `DataTable` `empty`.
  - Rows, cells and row groups carry their ARIA roles (`row`, `cell`, `columnheader`, `rowgroup`, `table`), since a table laid out as a grid can lose its native semantics in some browsers.

### Added

- **`pnpm verify --browsers <n>`: one run holds at most `n` browsers at once** ([#77](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/77)).
  - Without the flag, the ceiling is one browser per two CPUs and per 4 GiB of memory, whichever is fewer. `--full`'s audit, presses, keyboard walk and product checks start under the ceiling, not all at once.
  - A check in `browserChecks` holds one browser, or the number its entry names: `{ script, browsers }`, the whole ceiling at most. It starts once that many are free and is told in `VERIFY_BROWSERS` how many it holds, so a check that pools browsers sizes its pool from that number, not from the machine. The sample's journey drives three people at once and declares `browsers: 3`.
  - The trace header prints the ceiling used, for example `browsers: at most 5 at once (10 CPUs and 32 GiB)`.
  - Without a ceiling, one run on a 32 GB laptop held about 70 Chrome processes and pushed the machine about 17 GB into swap.

## [0.25.1] · 2026-10-21

### Fixed

- **A right-aligned or centred cut cell keeps its alignment.** Since 0.25.0 a `truncate` cell's value sits in a grid track as wide as the value, and the cell's `text-align` does not move a track, so a numeric or right-aligned `truncate` cell drew its value at the left. `Cut` takes `align` now and `TableCell` and `DataTable` pass the cell's own; a hand-built cell that uses `Cut` in a right-aligned column passes `align="right"`.
- **The audit's title check reaches a title column with phone words.** A cut name inside a link and a flex row in a column with `mobileCell` sits seven levels under the cell that carries its title, one more than the walk read, so the audit reported the name as cut without its whole text. The walk reads eight levels.

## [0.25.0] · 2026-10-21

### Breaking

- **Every table column shares the spare width, and `TableHeader`'s `flexible` prop is removed.** A cut cell used to zero its own width (`max-w-0`) and the lead head claimed all the rest (`w-full`), so the record's name sat in empty room while the columns beside it were held at their minimum, cramped at both ends and wrapping. Now every column asks for its content width and a table with room to spare shares it across all of its columns in proportion to those widths. A column whose cells `truncate` asks for its whole content width too and shrinks only when the table lacks room for every column; a column given `width` keeps that width and takes no spare; the `grow` column keeps its floor, 160px, 256px once the table is 672px wide. Drop `flexible` from any `TableHeader`: a `truncate` column now asks for its own width. Before 1.0, Meridian marks a breaking change with the minor version, as semver allows for 0.x.

### Added

- **`Cut`**, exported from `@/components/ui/table`: the wrapper a cut cell renders, one grid track of `minmax(0, max-content)` around a `truncate` span. `TableCell truncate` and `DataTable`'s `truncate` columns use it; use it in a hand-built cell that should be cut the same way. The audit's title check reaches through it.

## [0.24.0] · 2026-10-20

### Added

- **First-load JS can be held to each route's baseline alone** ([#76](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/76)), as every page's HTML is since 0.19.0. Set `budgets.firstLoadKb: null` in `scripts/verify.config.ts`: a route `scripts/verify.baseline.json` holds is then held to `firstLoadGrowthPct` over its own figure and nothing else, and the 820 KiB cap binds only a route the baseline does not hold yet. A product whose largest page sits near the cap no longer fails `pnpm verify` for a change its own baseline allows, and no longer derives a cap from the baseline to say the same rule twice. Leaving the key out keeps the cap on every route, as before; the size line says which applies (`cap 820 KiB for a route the baseline does not hold`).

## [0.23.0] · 2026-10-20

### Added

- **`Prose` names each body cell's column, and stacks a narrow table into labelled cards** ([#75](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/75)). Every body `td` carries its column's heading as `data-label`, written while the markdown is rendered, so it is in the server's HTML and a page can style a narrow table from `td[data-label]` on the first paint, with no second markdown parser. `stack` does it for you: a table under 480px of its own width reads as one card per row, the header row hidden, the first cell the title and each later cell under its column's heading. Wider, it is the same table; without `stack`, nothing changes.

## [0.22.0] · 2026-10-20

### Added

- **A tile whose figure is a share reads its change in points** ([#74](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/74)). `MetricTile` takes `deltaFormat` and hands it to `Delta`, whose `format` now takes a name as well as a function: `percent` (the default, a share of the earlier value) or `points`, so a share that went from 33.0% to 46.4% reads "▲13.4 pts" (`delta={0.134}`) with the same arrow, colour and screen-reader word, where it could only read "▲40.6%". `DeltaFormat` and `DELTA_FORMATS` are exported from `@/components/ui/delta`.

### Fixed

- **A Delta with no change reads its format** (#74): "0.0 pts" under `points`, or what a function makes of zero, where it printed a literal "0%" whatever the format. With no format given it still reads "0%".

## [0.21.0] · 2026-10-20

### Added

- **`TrendChart` takes `whole`** ([#73](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/73)), as `ColumnChart` does since 0.20.0: the values are counts, a stack of counts too, and every level is a whole number, so a running total whose top is 10 draws no level at 2.5 or 7.5. Pass it on a chart of counts; a chart of a quantity that is not one keeps its halves.

## [0.20.0] · 2026-10-20

### Added

- **The assistant panel offers the page's questions** ([#69](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/69)). `SharedContext.questions` holds the few questions a person would ask first about what a view shows, in their words. While the thread is empty, the console's assistant offers them under its line, as buttons under "Questions to start with", and pressing one sends it as the person's own. A page that shares none keeps the line alone, and the model is never told them. The sample's Home offers questions only about figures the reader may read.
- **`ColumnChart` takes `whole`** ([#72](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/72)) for a chart of counts: every level is a whole number, so a top of 1 is ticked 0 and 1, with no unlabelled gridline at 0.5. `niceTicks` and `ticksBetween` in `@/components/charts/scale` take the same as a last argument.
- **`ActivityEvent.unknown`** ([#68](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/68)) says the record does not hold who acted. The feed draws an empty disc in a dashed hairline instead of initials, so nobody unknown reads as a person, and the line still reads the product's words for them (`actor`, such as "Someone not recorded").

### Changed

- **A toned stacked chart tells two usual parts apart** ([#67](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/67)). Where a part already is the neutral (`tone: 'neutral'`), a part with neither a tone nor a slot, Other among them, is drawn in the quieter `chart-neutral`, where it was `chart-neutral-strong` like the neutral beside it. Other beside categorical slots is unchanged. To keep two usual parts apart, give the one that is the usual case `tone: 'neutral'` and leave the other untoned.
- **The column drawn last takes the card's padding when the last column is hidden** ([#70](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/70)). The Table padded the last cell of every row, so where the last column's `hideBelow` dropped it, the column drawn last kept `px-4` and its cells ran past the card's padding edge. Head and body cells with `hideBelow` now carry `data-hide-below`, and the Table pads the cell drawn last at each width. A cell you write yourself with `hideBelow` passes it to `TableCell` or `TableHeader`, as before.
- **A running period with no data reads "So far" where its column is too narrow** ([#71](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/71)). A Column chart labelled it "No data so far" and cut that to "No data…" in a phone's column, which reads as no data. At rest, in a column too narrow for the words, it reads "So far" above the dashed outline; pointed at, in the readout, the announcement and the table it says them whole.
- **The assistant panel's Clear and Close show their words, and on a phone the sheet is the whole width** (#69). Clear and Close are small ghost buttons with an icon and the word, still named "Clear conversation" and "Close assistant". Below `sm` the sheet covers the page: the 48px strip of cut page words it left beside it is gone. Escape and Close still close it.

## [0.19.3] · 2026-10-19

### Fixed

- **The presses suite finds a control again when its name has gained a live count** ([#66](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/66)). `ShellTools` names its button `Alerts`, then `Alerts, 3 new` once its read answers; on a loaded machine the first load was listed before that read and the fresh load after it, so 0.19.2's find-by-name looked for `Alerts`, waited out the hang bound and reported "was listed but could not be found again". When the exact name is gone, the suite now finds the control by the part of its name before the first comma, and which of the controls that share it it is. Names stay as they are: a count in the accessible name is still how a screen reader hears it.

## [0.19.2] · 2026-10-19

### Fixed

- **The presses suite finds a control again by what it is** ([#65](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/65)). It tagged each control by its place in the walk and, after opening the route again, pressed whatever stood at that place: a later load that drew the controls in another order, or fewer of them for a while (a table still loading on a busy machine, rows another suite's write changed), pressed the wrong control or reported "was listed but could not be found again" for one that was on the page a moment later. A control is now found by its accessible name, and which of the controls named alike it is, and one not drawn yet is waited for up to the hang bound before it is called gone.

## [0.19.1] · 2026-10-19

### Fixed

- **The presses suite no longer crashes when a press replaces the document late** ([#64](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/64)). A control whose answer reloads the page once a request is accepted, a team switch that has the server draw the new team's chrome, could replace the document after `scripts/interactions.ts` had looked for an answer; its next read of the page then found no helper and stopped the whole suite with `ReferenceError: deepQuery is not defined`. A document replaced after the look is a press that moved the page: the suite opens the route again and presses the next control, and the press is not reported as doing nothing.

## [0.19.0] · 2026-10-19

### Breaking

- **Every page's HTML is held to its own baseline, and measured gzipped** ([#57](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/57), [#56](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/56)). `scripts/sizes.ts` measures a page's HTML as a person downloads it, the complete streamed response gzipped, where it measured the uncompressed bytes of the routes in `budgets.htmlKb` only. `node scripts/sizes.ts --write-baseline --base <url>`, against the running build, records the HTML of every page (no API, no dynamic segment, not the not-found screen) beside the first-load figures in `scripts/verify.baseline.json`, and `pnpm verify` holds each to `budgets.htmlGrowthPct` (5%) over its own figure, as first-load JS is held. A growth you accept is recorded once, with the same command, rather than by editing numbers. `budgets.htmlKb` stays as an optional absolute cap, now in gzipped KiB: a cap set on uncompressed bytes is several times too loose, so lower it to the gzipped figure `pnpm verify` prints, or remove it and rely on the baseline. Until the baseline holds `html`, the HTML line is a warning, and `pnpm verify --full` refuses, naming the command. Under `partialPrefetching` Next.js writes each shell's static trees into a page's HTML twice; gzipped, the second copy costs a few KiB rather than tens, so the figure follows the page.

### Added

- **A stacked Column chart's part can take a categorical slot** ([#61](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/61)). `ColumnPart.color` (1 to 6) draws the part in `series-<n>`, as `TrendSeries.color` does, for parts that are identities, such as teams, or for two or more usual outcomes, which one neutral would make one. A part with no slot, Other among them, is neutral, and status colours stay out of such a chart: coral and saffron read as a warning beside a real one. `docs/data-display.md`'s Parts of one whole says when to use it.
- **`DataTable` takes `retrying`** ([#59](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/59)): its error state's Retry is busy while the person's retry is on its way. Pass it for their own press only.
- **`ActivityEvent.href`** ([#63](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/63)) links an event's object to its record, with the arrow that says it stays in the product.
- **`Rail` takes `declared`**, every destination the product declares; `ConsoleRail` passes it from the whole navigation.

### Fixed

- **A busy Button keeps its focus** ([#58](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/58)). It was `disabled`, so a focused button that turned busy from anything but its own press, a Retry whose read a live hint or a poll started, lost focus to the page and the next Tab began again at the top. It is `aria-disabled` now, stays in the tab order, and drops a press while busy, a submit's included; `aria-busy` and the spinner are unchanged. A test of yours that expects a busy button to be `disabled` expects `aria-disabled="true"`.
- **A Column chart's period with no data is no longer its tallest mark** ([#60](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/60)). It drew a `fill-track` track the height of the scale, which in light theme read as the biggest period. It draws no column now, a dashed hairline just above the baseline, and its word ("No data") stands over it where a value would. The hairline carries `data-no-data-mark` where the track carried `data-track`. `npx zz-meridian@latest update` lists `migration:no-data-mark` for a project whose copy of `tests/column-chart-no-data.test.tsx` still expects the track: bring the release's over.
- **The rail lights nothing for an address under a destination the person does not hold** ([#62](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/62)). It lit the longest destination they held, so someone refused `/admin/audit` saw `/admin` lit. The current page is found among every declared destination and lit only when their rail holds it. A product that passed `current=''` to work around it can stop.
- **A Button's class list is 178 bytes, not 394** ([#56](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/56)): the frame every button shares is the `.btn` class in `src/styles/base.css`, so a table of row actions does not repeat it on every instance.

## [0.18.1] · 2026-10-19

### Changed

- **Next.js 16.4.2**, from 16.3.8. 16.4.1 carries fixes for two critical advisories, remote code execution through a `use cache` function that returns a typed array (GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr) and through the Image Optimization API with SVG files (GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw), and a high one, denial of service through Server Actions (GHSA-p3m5-59mp-jv5r); 16.3 has no release that carries them. `npx zz-meridian@latest update` raises `next` and `eslint-config-next` to `16.4.2` in a project's `package.json`; run your package manager's install after it.
- **The other dependencies, within their majors**: React 19.3.1, `ai` 7.0.143 with `@ai-sdk/react` 4.0.147, `@ai-sdk/anthropic` 4.0.82 and `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible` 3.0.71, `radix-ui` 1.8.1, `lucide-react` 1.59.0, `zod` 4.6.7, and for development `vitest` 5.0.5, `jsdom` 30.1.3 and `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.1.2.
- **`@testing-library/jest-dom` 7**, which needs `@testing-library/dom` as a dependency of the project's own. `adopt` and `update` add `@testing-library/dom` `^10.4.2` to `devDependencies`; `update` raises jest-dom to 7 where Meridian wrote it and asks you to where you chose your own. The matchers are unchanged.
- TypeScript stays at 5.9 and ESLint at 9: `typescript-eslint` supports TypeScript below 6.1, and `eslint-plugin-react`, `eslint-plugin-import` and `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`, which `eslint-config-next` installs, do not yet declare ESLint 10.

### Fixed

- **`update` and `adopt` install where `CI` is set.** Both change `package.json` and then run the project's install, and pnpm freezes the lockfile where `CI` is set, so an update that raised a dependency failed with `ERR_PNPM_OUTDATED_LOCKFILE` in CI or under an agent that sets it. With pnpm they now run `pnpm install --no-frozen-lockfile`; npm, yarn and bun run `install` as before.
- **A client navigation no longer reads the new page's collections again** ([#54](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/54)). 0.18.0 sent the revisions a page was served only on the tab's first stream, so a press in the rail to a page that follows other collections reopened the stream without them, and every collection that page follows was read by its server and again by the stream's resync. `src/lib/live.ts` now sends them on every open that follows a change of the names, and only a reopen after a gap (an error, a tab shown again, the poll's retry) resyncs every name. A created project takes the fix with `npx zz-meridian@latest update`.

## [0.18.0] · 2026-10-19

### Breaking

- **The phone the timed suites emulate is one device on every host: the device of Lighthouse benchmark index 800**, the top of Lighthouse's Mid-Tier Mobile bracket. `scripts/vitals.ts` and the navigation journey (`pnpm verify --timed` and `--perf`) measure the host's own index once per run (Lighthouse's `computeBenchmarkIndex`, the median of three readings) and slow the CPU by that index over 800, to one decimal and never below 1, where they slowed it four times on every host. A host whose index is above 3,200 is now slowed more than before and one below it less, so a laptop and a runner whose CPU varies between runs are held to the same phone. Each timed suite prints `phone: this host slowed <rate>x (benchmark index <index> …)`, and `pnpm verify` keeps that line under its navigation and Web Vitals phases. The floors and the budgets are unchanged. A near miss that used to pass on a fast machine is a page to speed up, not a budget to move.
- **The desktop prefetch cap counts every router prefetch.** It counted only requests carrying `next-router-prefetch: 1`, while Next 16 sends `3` for the runtime shell every link prefetches and `2` for a full prefetch's URL data, so it read a fraction of what the router fetched: Meridian's Home reads 45.3 KiB where it read 8.8 KiB. A project whose landing page now reads over its `budgets.prefetchKb.desktop` either prefetches less from that page or sets the cap from its own measurement.
- **The whole 20-sample protocol runs only in CI, split by route across runners.** `pnpm verify --perf` and `node scripts/perf.ts` without `--routes` now refuse where `CI` is not `true`, before the gate or Chrome starts: the protocol is 1,800 journeys, an hour and a half of a machine, and a person's machine is never quiet for that long. In CI, split it with `--shard <i>/<n>` (`pnpm verify --perf --shard 2/5`, with `--perf` alone; the shards together run every rail route once), one shard a runner. On your own machine, narrow it to the route in question: `node scripts/perf.ts --base <url> --routes <route>`. A script or agent prompt that ran `pnpm verify --perf` locally runs it in CI instead.

### Added

- **A project's own check that judges a time has a tier of its own** ([#48](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/48)). `timedChecks` in `scripts/verify.config.ts` lists browser checks that judge a time: `pnpm verify --timed` runs each with `--base`, alone, after the live data and before Web Vitals, and reports them as the `timedChecks` suite. A check that measured a budget inside `browserChecks`, under the other suites' load, moves to `timedChecks`.
- **The fake model can call a project's own tools** ([#49](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/49)). `fakeModel` in `scripts/verify.config.ts` lists `{ when, tool, input }` steps: for a question matching `when` (a case-insensitive pattern) the fake model calls `tool` with `input` when the request hands it, so a journey can check that the assistant cites what the page shows. A pattern that does not compile fails `pnpm verify` before the gate.
- **`scripts/shot.ts --as <persona>`** ([#50](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/50)) signs each page in with that persona's cookie from `scripts/verify.config.ts` and names each file `<route>-<persona>-<width>-<theme>.png`, so a second persona's pages can be shot for a review. An undeclared persona is refused before Chrome starts, and any option of `shot.ts` given no value (`--as` last on the line) is refused rather than shot without it.

### Changed

- **The start guide keeps the shared phone budget.** `/system/start/start-a-dashboard` carried its own phone-shell budget of 300 ms in `scripts/verify.config.ts`, against the shared 250, because its heading measured 243 to 282 ms on the phone. Since the rail's Docs item prefetches the page in full, its warm phone shell reads 28 ms on the machine that set the exception and 59 ms on the scheduled runner, so the exception is gone. A project's own `budget` on a navigation check works as before.
- **The fake model refuses the key only on the word `fail`.** It refused any message containing `fail`, so a question such as "Which runs failed?" could not be a `fakeModel` step; "Please fail now" is still refused.
- **Meridian's weekly CI runs each tier as its own job.** `full`, `timed`, `perf` (five shards of the rail routes) and `consumer` run in parallel, each on its own runner and each within half an hour, where one job ran `pnpm verify --full --timed --perf` for over two hours. A red is rerun by its job alone, and a dispatch can name the jobs it runs (`gh workflow run weekly.yml -f jobs=timed`). `docs/testing.md` says a project's own CI runs the perf protocol the same way.
- **A dropped frame says where its time went.** `scripts/vitals.ts`'s line for a page that drops a frame ends with the longest gap and, from the Long Animation Frames API, how much of it was script (and whose), style and layout, and render, or that no long animation frame covered it, which points at the compositor or the host rather than the page: `longest gap 167 ms: a long animation frame of 185 ms (script 181 ms by BUTTON.onclick, style and layout 0 ms, render 0 ms)`.

### Fixed

- **`tests/explore-runner.test.ts` no longer fails on a busy machine.** Its large-collection case built a million rows to show that a prepared query asks one aggregate and reads no page, which took 2.8 s alone and passed the 30 s test timeout with the other test workers on a loaded host. It builds 120,000 now, and asserts the same calls. A created project keeps its own copy of the test: bring the release's over, or change `1_000_000` to `120_000` and `'750,010'` to `'90,000'`.
- **Opening the phone drawer and changing a period no longer lay the page out inside the tap.** Opening the navigation drawer focused its first link inside the tap and let the modal's changes to `<body>` (`pointer-events: none`, the scroll lock's scrollbar width) restyle the whole page, and a period change made the segmented control read its option's position inside the same task: each forced the browser to lay out the page before the frame, which dropped a frame of 67 to 118 ms on the scheduled runner's phone. The drawer stays a modal dialog: focus moves into it a frame after it opens and returns to the button that opened it, the shell's frame stops the inherited changes, and the segmented control measures its thumb in a `ResizeObserver` callback, after layout. A frame that holds a drawer (`data-drawer-frame`) now owns its `pointer-events` while the drawer is open. On the scheduled runner, whose style and layout run about twice as slow as its benchmark index says, some pages still drop a frame: the drawer's mount script and the period's re-render are the work left.
- **A live page no longer reads its collections again right after it loads** ([#53](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/53)). The stream's first open re-read every collection the server had just served, so a page reading one path from two components and the stream asked for it three times a load, and under a slow API those reads held the browser's connections while the next navigation waited. A collection now has an optional `revision()`; `read`, `readAll` and `aggregate` return the revision they read at, a page hands its views those revisions, and the stream's first open sends them as `since`: the stream greets `synced` when nothing moved, and `resync` naming only what did, so no write between the server's read and the stream's subscription is lost. An adapter without `revision()` is resynced on every open, as before.
- **The standard Metric tile keeps a long label in its title** ([#51](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/51)), as the compact tile does, so the audit no longer reports a cut label through this managed file.
- **A toggle probe reads the whole of what it watches** ([#52](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/52)). The navigation compared the first 80 characters of each element its `resultSelector` matches, so a control that changes the end of a long row (an inline confirm in its last cell) was reported dead. A `resultSelector` that holds a ticking clock now counts as changing, so point it at what the control changes.
- **The console rail's Docs link finds the start guide ready.** The rail's Docs item asks for its page in full (`NavItem.prefetch`, a new optional field), since the start guide's article is its page's URL data, which the default App Shell prefetch leaves for after the press: from `/settings` the start guide's desktop shell fell from 77-146 ms to about 30 ms, and on the phone from 262 to 24 ms, at the cost of one prefetch of about 15 KiB on each main-workspace page on the desktop. The rail keeps Next's default for every other item, and compiles in a project whose `NavItem` has no such field. `scripts/live.ts` tells a prefetch from the router's own read by the same rule as the cap.
- **`node scripts/audit.ts --atlas` no longer reports overlaps on the Atlas pattern pages** (the 0.17.0 known issue). The audit measured `content-visibility: auto` subtrees the browser had not laid out yet; it now renders each such subtree before it measures and waits two frames. A `content-visibility: hidden` subtree is left alone, since it is not drawn.
- **The presses follow a relative link as a browser does.** `scripts/interactions.ts` skipped every `href` that did not start with `/`, so a relative link that answered 404 passed. It now resolves each link against its page (`linkPath` in `scripts/lib/links.ts`), follows every link to the page's own site, and reports `link <href> (<resolved path>) answers <status>`.
- **The Services page's uptime strip ends on today in `app.timezone`.** Its days were cut at the UTC date, so east of UTC after local midnight (west of UTC in the evening) the line's last day was a day off from the periods every other part of the page cuts with `dayOf`. A product whose `app.timezone` is UTC sees no change.
- **The checks no longer fill your Downloads folder.** Every press of an Export button (Home, Revenue and Usage) is a real download, and the browser `scripts/lib/chrome.ts` launches had no download directory of its own, so each `pnpm verify --full` and `--as` saved their CSVs in the person's Downloads folder, outside the temporary profile the run removes. Downloads now go inside that profile and are removed with it. Files an earlier run left there are named `home-<period>.csv`, `revenue-<period>.csv` and `usage-<period>.csv`, with ` (1)`, ` (2)` and so on after them, and can be deleted.
- **The workspace journeys read a page's heading once the page is there.** `journeys/workspaces.ts` read the heading as soon as the address and the rail were right, which on a loaded machine could be while the page's `loading.tsx` still stood in for it, whose masthead holds the page's name where the record's will be: the customer step failed with `found a page headed "Customer:"`. It now waits while a busy `role="status"` is on the page. A project that kept the sample's journeys brings `journeys/workspaces.ts` over from the release.
- **The `agent-does-here-section` update item finds a view tool kept in a module of its own.** It read `src/views/tools.ts` alone, so a project updating across 0.16.0 whose tools sat in modules the registry imports was told of no page while the gate, which reads those modules since 0.17.0, failed them. It now reads every module under `src/`.
- **A page specification's Theme control no longer drops a frame on a phone** (the 0.17.0 known issue). The stage keyed each pane by its theme, so a Dark-to-Light tap unmounted the pane and loaded the whole page again in a new frame inside the tap. Each pane is now keyed by its position: the frame on screen stays mounted and is re-dressed.

### Known issues

- **On Meridian's scheduled runner, `pnpm verify --timed` drops one frame on two pages**: opening `/business`'s phone drawer and changing `/`'s period, in style and layout of 49 and 64 ms that the benchmark index does not calibrate (2 of 30 pages). Both pass on a quiet machine at the standard's phone, and no floor moved for them, so the weekly `timed` job stays red until the pages do less work in the tap. `pnpm verify` and `--full` are not affected.

## [0.17.0] · 2026-10-18

### Breaking

- **`pnpm verify --full` no longer judges a time.** It runs the complete suites: every rail route navigated for its function, the audit, every control pressed and link followed, the keyboard walk, the assistant and the product's own browser checks. The navigation budgets, the live-data suite and Web Vitals moved to the new `pnpm verify --timed`, which runs them alone and last and needs a quiet machine. Only `pnpm verify --full --timed` can end `the project meets the Meridian standard`; a `--full` run with nothing else unrun ends `every check that ran passed; not run: timing, live, vitals; pnpm verify --timed runs them`. A script, workflow or agent prompt that ran `--full` for the whole standard runs `--full --timed` instead; weekly CI runs `pnpm verify --full --timed --perf`. `docs/testing.md` classifies every check by size, type and tier.
- **`pnpm verify` and `--full` no longer fail a route for being slow.** Their navigation (`scripts/navigate.ts --timing off`) judges whether each route's shell, data and first interaction arrive, never how fast; `pnpm verify --timed` judges the same journeys against `budgets.navigation`. A project that relied on the default smoke to catch a slow route runs `pnpm verify --timed` on a quiet machine.
- **`pnpm gate` no longer runs `tests/deep-*`**, the browser-fixture tests of the checks, which only this repository has. Its closing line names the command that does: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/deep- --no-file-parallelism`.
- **The coverage line has new words.** Its depth can be `timed`, `full+timed` or `full+timed+perf`, and its `not run:` list can name `timing`. A script that matched `coverage: full;` for a complete run matches `coverage: full+timed;` and `not run: none`.
- **`finalOutcome` (`scripts/lib/coverage.ts`) takes `{ full, timed }` in place of a boolean.** A project's own copy of `tests/verify-suite-outcome.test.ts` from before the tiers calls `finalOutcome(true, …)` and failed its update's gate at the type check; the update now reports it as the `verify-tiers` migration. Bring `tests/verify-suite-outcome.test.ts` over from the release, and call `finalOutcome({ full, timed }, notRun)` in a file of your own.

### Added

- **A Featured metric can say its period has no number** ([#45](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/45)). `FeaturedFigure.value` is `number | null`, and `points` takes `null` entries. With no number, the figure's line holds `missing`, the caller's words ("No words recorded", "Unavailable"; "Not recorded" by default), a size down and at the figure's own height, so a period without a number draws the same card as one with. A pointed point that is `null` reads the same way. Before this, the only ways through were a false `0`, or `NaN` with a `format` that turned it into a word; a card that did the second can pass `null` and `missing` now.

### Changed

- **`docs/testing.md` is the testing standard.** Above its classification it says what kinds of test there are (size and type), the tiers that combine them, when each runs (while editing, before a commit, before finishing and handing over a change, before a release, when a given kind of change is made, and on a schedule), and the rules a red, a limit and a release follow, with the practice they rest on. A project is advised to follow it and Meridian follows it. `AGENTS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/distribution.md` and `references/validation.md` point to it instead of keeping their own account, and `tests/cli-testing-standard.test.ts` keeps its shape.

### Fixed

- **The assistant is told it changes nothing where it is handed no write tool** ([#46](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/46)). The system prompt always said "Your write tools change what this page shows…" and that the person approves each Proposal, even on a page whose collections the assistant may only read, where no write tool is built and no Proposal can follow. `respond` now tells `systemPrompt` whether it handed a write tool (`Audience.writes`). Without one, the prompt says the assistant changes nothing and, asked for any change, gives the page that makes it, or says it is not a change the person can make. With one, it keeps both lines. The shared guidance that no change exists unless a write tool returned one stays in both.
- **`ask` writes one transcript a page and can ask a detail page on the record you name** ([#47](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/47)). A page with no view tool was named by its folder, a detail page by the folder before its `[id]`, so `/things` and `/things/[id]` both wrote `things.md` and the second overwrote the first. A page without a tool is now named by its whole route (`/things/[id]` is `things-id`), two views that would share a name are told apart by their routes, and a name still shared is refused before anything is asked. `--view` takes the assistant's tool name as well (`view_usage`), and `--path <address>` asks the page that renders that address on that record, rather than on the first row its listing draws, which may be a closed one. The view names from tools defined in modules the registry imports came with #43. A transcript of a page without a tool has a new file name: compare the new file with the old one, under its new name.
- **Pointing at a Featured metric's chart moves nothing below the figure** ([#44](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/44)). A figure given `points` and no `delta` drew the row under the figure only while a point was pointed at, so the card grew by the point pill's row as the pointer crossed its chart and shrank when it left: measured at 390 px, 380 → 426 px, and a control below moved 46 px with it, enough for a press aimed at it to land on something else. The row is now drawn at rest as a line of its own, holding the pill's place empty, so the card stays 426 px either way however long the point's name.
- **The gate and `ask` find a view tool kept in a module of its own** ([#43](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/43)). `viewToolPages()` (`scripts/lib/routes.ts`) read the `defineViewTool` calls written in `src/views/tools.ts` alone, so a project that keeps each tool in a module the registry imports got an empty list. `scripts/check.ts` then asked no page for its `### What the agent does here` — green by absence — and `scripts/ask.ts` named every view by its page's folder, never by its tool. It now reads every project module the registry reaches through its imports, by a relative path or a tsconfig alias, an `index` included. A project whose tools sat in such modules can find pages failing `check.ts` that passed before: each is a page a tool names whose specification lacks the part, and the fix is that part.
- **Home and Revenue are live pages.** Each body registers the collections its feature needs with `useLive`, so `/` and `/business` resync on open, on reconnect and on the safety poll, and `pnpm verify --perf` finds an after-live start for every Home and Business route again: since the rails became per workspace, `/members`, `/customers`, `/usage` and `/requests` had none (`no other rail route registers live data to start from`).
- **A link probe is pressed once the destination's data is measured.** The navigation measured the data and pressed the probe side by side, so on a route whose link is ready before its ready text (`/business`, while its title still loaded) the press could leave before the data counted, and a sample failed `main is there but never shows`. A dead link is still reported.
- **Switching an Atlas card's theme no longer drops a frame on a phone.** The stage's specimen sections are skipped by style and layout until they near the viewport (`content-visibility: auto`), so the Theme, Accent, Density and Width switches restyle only what is on screen; a chart pattern's specimen holds thousands of elements, and the switch cost one frame of 100 to 160 ms on a mid-range phone.
- **`pnpm verify --perf` holds a route to the budget its navigation check carries**, as `pnpm verify --timed` already did. A route's own figure (`budget` on its check in `verify.config.ts`) now stands in for the shared warm figure in both suites, through one function (`limitsFor` in `scripts/lib/budgets.ts`), so the two no longer judge one route against two bars: the start guide's phone warm shell is reported against its own 300 ms, not the shared 250 ms. Cold and after-live keep the shared figures.
- **The Atlas's Contributing page no longer links to a page that answers 404.** It linked `docs/testing.md` relatively, which resolved to `/system/system/docs/testing.md`; the file is now named in backticks, as every other Atlas document names a file, and `tests/cli-atlas-doc-links.test.ts` keeps every document the Atlas serves free of relative links.

### Known issues

- **`node scripts/audit.ts --atlas` reports overlaps on the Atlas pattern pages whose specimen renders with `content-visibility: auto`** (featured metric, metric tile, column chart, heatmap and the records template). The pages draw nothing overlapping: the audit measures sections the browser has not laid out yet. Fixed in the next release. `pnpm verify` and its tiers are not affected.
- **`node scripts/vitals.ts --atlas` can drop one frame on a page specification's look control** (`/system/pages/revenue`, one run in three), where the stage restyles its console and phone frames in one tap. Fixed in the next release. `pnpm verify --timed` is not affected.


## [0.16.3] · 2026-10-17

### Breaking

- **The navigation journey moved to `scripts/lib/journey.ts`.** A created project's `tests/navigate-start.test.ts` and `tests/navigate-menu-start.test.ts` import `startFor` and `journeyStart` from `scripts/navigate.ts`, which no longer has them, so they fail the type check until they are brought over. The files are the project's own, so the update does not write them: it reports them as the migration `journey-moved` — bring the release's copies over, and import the journey (`journey`, `journeyStart`, `startFor`, `drawerRoutes`, `prepare`, `liveRefresh`, `Fail` and its types) from `scripts/lib/journey.ts` in a file of your own. `scripts/navigate.ts` keeps the command and `workspaceHomes`.

### Changed

- **The default verify does less twice, and the gate runs its three long checks side by side.** `scripts/gate.ts` runs the type check, the lint and the tests together once the quick checks before them pass, and prints their lines in the same order as before. All three run to the end, so one failure never hides another. The build inside `pnpm verify` no longer type-checks the tree a second time, because the gate has just checked it with the same tsconfig: verify sets `MERIDIAN_TYPES_CHECKED` for that build, and the template's `next.config.ts` reads it as `typescript.ignoreBuildErrors`. Every other `next build` still checks types. An adopted project keeps its own `next.config`, so its verify checks types twice as before, unless it adds that line. Measured from a clean tree on one machine: the gate 22.9 → 15.4 s, the build 14.5 → 9.5 s, the default verify 62.2 → 49.8 s. The release's verify had reached 193 s against its 180 s limit on the slowest runner CPU.
- **Every source file Meridian ships is under 700 lines** ([#41](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/41)). `scripts/navigate.ts` had grown to 775, the only managed file past a ceiling a project may hold its own files to — and a project cannot split a managed file, because the update restores it. The journey and its measurements are `scripts/lib/journey.ts` now (467 lines) and `scripts/navigate.ts` keeps the command and its report (319); nothing either does changed. A test of the package holds every shipped file under `src`, `app`, `scripts` and `tests` to 700 lines, so a release cannot grow one past it again.

### Fixed

- **Today is the product's day wherever a day is cut** ([#42](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/42)). The assistant's prompt told the model today in `app.timezone`, while the prepared query (`today` in `app/api/assistant/route.ts`) and the pages' periods (`readDays` in `src/data/metrics.ts`) cut their days at the UTC date. So east of UTC after local midnight, and west of it in the evening, the model's today and the end of its `7d` window were a day apart, and a day row — kept under its day in `app.timezone` — fell on the wrong side of the cut. **`dayOf(at)`** (`src/lib/format-date.ts`) is the calendar day of an instant in `app.timezone`, and the route, `readDays` and the sample's views (Now's month and its runs by day, Revenue's ladder and trials, a customer's busiest day and trial days left, a new member's joined date) ask it now. A product whose `app.timezone` is UTC sees no change. The route and `src/data/metrics.ts` are the team's own files, so the update reports them as the migration `today-in-timezone` — replace `clock().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` with `dayOf(clock())`.
- **A view whose page is a catch-all route is handed its prepared query at every depth** ([#40](https://github.com/zhixuan312/zz-meridian/issues/40)). The assistant route matched a view's `page` against the address segment by segment and required the two to have as many segments, reading `[...name]` as any one segment and `[[...name]]` as one too: a view on `/docs/[...path]` was the page at `/docs/a` and not at `/docs/a/b`, which the same Next.js route renders, and one on `/docs/[[...path]]` was never the page at `/docs`. The match is `viewAt` in `src/lib/shared-context.ts` now, and it matches as Next.js does — `[...name]` one or more segments, `[[...name]]` zero or more — and where two views match one address it takes the one Next.js renders: a static segment over `[name]`, that over a catch-all. The route asks whether the person may use that view after choosing it, so a less specific view never stands in for the page. `app/api/assistant/route.ts` is the team's own file, so the update does not write it: it reports it as the migration `view-page-matched-as-next` — delete the route's `onPage` and find the view with `viewAt`. `node scripts/ask.ts` asks a catch-all page's questions on a link of any depth, and an optional catch-all's on the page with none.

## [0.16.2] · 2026-10-16

### Breaking

- **A created project's shipped test of the rail fails until it is brought over.** `tests/rail-marker.test.tsx` — the template's test of the rail's marker — stands over a `ResizeObserver` whose `observe` answers with nothing, which only worked because the rail read its own offsets as well. That read is what 0.16.2 removed (see **Changed**), so the file now asserts a marker position that nothing has measured yet. The file is the project's own from the moment it is created, so the update does not write it: it reports it as the migration `marker-measured-on-observe`, with the fix — bring the release's `tests/rail-marker.test.tsx` over as the release has it. Its fake answers `observe` with the callback and reads the marker after that delivery; a test of your own that mounts the rail answers `observe` the same way.
- **The Web Vitals check fails on a dropped frame.** `scripts/vitals.ts` counted frames over 50 ms while its taps played out and printed the number on every line without ever failing on it, so a `verify --full` that passed was not being held to the floor its own header states. It fails now: `frames over 50 ms` must read `0 of N`. Two things about how it counts changed with it. Frames are read **in the tap's own window and no other** — the recording used to run from the moment the page opened, straight through the Escape that closes what a tap opened and the settle after it, which the INP measurement in the same loop already excludes because it is the check's own work and not the person's. And a page that drops one frame is **measured a second time in full** before it is failed, because a single frame in two hundred is inside the noise of the host a four-times throttle is measuring: a page that drops one twice is a page that stutters. A run that was passing still passes; a run that was failing now says so, and names the control the frame was dropped on.

### Added

- **`useNavDrawer(asked)`** (`src/components/base/use-nav-drawer.ts`) — whether a navigation drawer is open, for the frame that owns one. It carries the bookkeeping both shells had written for themselves: the drawer opens for every press of the top bar's button that it has not answered yet, remembers the page it opened on so a navigation closes it, and forgets that page when the address moves. The Atlas's copy was missing the last of those, so opening the contents on a phone, following a link and pressing Back re-opened the drawer with nobody pressing anything. A product that wrote the same bookkeeping for a drawer of its own should read it from here.

### Changed

- **The rail reads its own geometry from the observer's first delivery, not by hand.** The rail measured itself with an `offsetTop` read as well as through its `ResizeObserver`, and a rail mounts inside the tap that opens the phone drawer — so that read made the browser lay the whole page out inside the tap: measured at a 50 ms frame on a throttled phone, against 33 ms for the mount alone. The observer's first delivery reports the same numbers after the browser has laid out for itself. Nothing moves while a measurement is missing: the current item draws the same pill itself.
- **The Atlas's front page names the latest release, not the work toward the next one.** It read the first section of `CHANGELOG.md` — which is `[Unreleased]` while a release is pending, and has no version — so the card showed pending work under the version it was going to be released as. It also read the version, the date and the summary with one pattern that refused the `#` of `### Breaking`, the first line under every heading in that file: the date fell to an empty fallback and the card printed `0.16.2 · ` with nothing after the separator, on every release. The heading and the paragraph are read separately now, and the "Full changelog" link no longer shares the kicker's line.
- **`AppMark` names its own gradient.** The mark is a client component now, because each draws a sheen whose id has to be its own: a page holds more than one mark (the Atlas draws one in the phone's bar and one in the shelf, both mounted at every width), and a fixed `id` was a duplicate on every one of those pages, with a `url(#…)` naming two elements. A product that overrode the mark's markup rather than its `app.logo` is unaffected; the first load of `/embed/now` in this template moved 809 → 811 KiB.

### Fixed

- **A new dashboard's own `pnpm verify` failed on a test that named the template.** `tests/embed-page-failure.test.tsx` asserts the embed's way out, and wrote the copy out as `Open in ZZ Meridian` — the template's name, while the frame draws `Open in ${app.name}`. Every dashboard is created under its own name, so the test passed in this repository and failed in every project made from it, at the first `pnpm verify` a team runs. It reads the name from `src/app.config.ts` now.

## [0.16.1] · 2026-10-16

### Breaking

- **The literal-colour rule reads more forms.** `scripts/check.ts` matched `#rrggbb`, `#rgb`, `rgb(`/`rgba(` and `hsl(`/`hsla(`. It now reads a hex of four or eight digits too — `#0a5F`, `#0a58caFF` — and `oklch()`, `oklab()`, `lab()`, `lch()` and `color()`. A gate that passed can fail on a line that writes one of them: use a role or a token (`text-ink`, `border-line`, `var(--…)`), or mark the line `allow-literal-colour` where the colour is genuinely a literal of its own, as an embed's simulated host and a third party's brand are. A colour composed from tokens is not a literal and is not reported, so `color-mix(in oklch, var(--ink) 40%, transparent)` stays as it is.
- **A flag a command does not read is refused, not ignored.** `zz-meridian adopt --dry-run` — and any other flag named outside the command that reads it — exits 1, naming the command it belongs to. A CI step that passed one did nothing quietly; it fails now, which is what it should have done.
- **A figure nobody measured is `null`, not zero.** `RequestSummary.p95` and `RequestSummary.errorShare` (`src/data/requests.ts`) are `null` when the set is empty, and `MetricTile.points` is `(number | null)[]`; `NowData`'s per-model `p95` and `errorRate` are `null` for a model with no usage row. A page, a fixture or a test of yours that builds one of them adds the `null` case; a tile whose point is `null` draws a gap where it drew a fall to zero, and the request log says "not measured" where it said `0ms` and `0.0%`.
- **The audit's two truncated-value rules mean what the card contract says.** `scripts/audit.ts` accepted any `title` anywhere near a cut value without comparing it with the text, and read `aria-labelledby` as the attribute's own value instead of resolving the ids it names. Both compare now, so a `verify --full` that passed can fail: put the whole of a cut value in a `title` that *holds* it (a title of something else keeps none of it), and name a real element with `aria-labelledby` — an id that is nowhere is a control with no name. `CONTRIBUTING.md` promised both all along.
- **The backend gate refuses a data URL it cannot read the host of.** `unsafeBackend` (`scripts/verify.ts`) skipped a value it could not read the host of, which is how a DSN written without a scheme (`user:pass@db.prod.example.com:5432/app`), a password holding a `/` or a `#`, or a libpq line (`host=… user=…`) reached the presses. The authority is read with its credentials dropped, a value whose host still cannot be read is refused rather than skipped, and a host named in the query string is read the way libpq reads it — `postgres:///app?host=db.prod.example.com` reaches that host, since libpq takes the host from the query and lets it override the authority, dials `hostaddr` when both are named, keeps the last value of a keyword it is given twice, and treats `/…` as a Unix socket, which stays local. A file scheme that names a host is another machine's file (`file://db.prod.example.com/share/app.db`) or a database over the network (`libsql://…`) rather than a file of this machine's. A refusal names the way out: point the value at a local copy for the run, or set `allowRemoteData: true` in `scripts/verify.config.ts`.

### Added

- **`readAll(name, q)`** (`src/data/read.ts`) answers with every row a collection holds, asking the same question of every page: a list that counts, totals or claims "every" starts there, and a `limit` passed to it is ignored, because its answer means every row. `readEndpoints`, `readServices` and the incident read that joins each ticket go through it, and so do the Members and the Keys view tools and the three pages that list them: the endpoint list, the incidents the bell and Services count, every member, every key and every service are now all of them, where each was the first hundred. A page of yours that read one page of a collection and called it every row reads through `readAll` too.

### Changed

- **Home says no service is monitored yet.** A services read that succeeded and returned no rows had the page's title and its line say "every service is operational" while the page's own fact said "0 of 0 operational". Both say the empty state now, in the words the Services and Now pages already use.
- **`adopt` keeps a commented tsconfig beside the rewritten one.** `adopt` rewrites `tsconfig.json` to raise the target and add the `@meridian/*` path, and `JSON.stringify` cannot hold a comment or a trailing comma. When the team's file carries either, it is kept as `tsconfig.before.json` and the notes say so; a plain tsconfig gets no backup.
- **`brand` reports a flag it cannot use.** `brand --theme purple` printed an uncaught `Error` where `adopt` and `create` answer the same value with a sentence. All three report it the same way now, before anything is copied.
- **The standard names three motion loops.** The token layer defines three (`--dur-loop-spin`, `--dur-loop-shimmer`, `--dur-loop-pulse`) and the Motion floor and the Skeleton card named two. All three are named everywhere now, and `tests/motion-loops.test.ts` holds the count to the tokens.

### Fixed

- **A held-back answer agrees with itself.** The line an answer ends with when the 3,000-character cap holds part of it back read "1 more line are not shown."; it reads "1 more line is not shown.", and the row messages follow.
- **A resumed update keeps the lockfile preimage a killed install left behind.** `--resume` probed the project for the team's lockfile, so a lockfile a killed install had rewritten stood in as "ours" and an abort would have put a half-written file back in place of the team's. It comes from the session's backup now.
- **The prepared query's 3,000-character cap bounds the whole answer.** It bounded the drilled rows, the one part it could shorten, so a summary over budget by itself came back over.
- **`nav-needs` reads a nav item by its braces.** An item a team had spread over its keys was reported as missing a `needs` that was two lines below its `href`.
- **Copy says "Copied" only when the copy happened.** A refused clipboard write left the button saying it had copied.
- **The short date is one function.** `formatShortDate` (`01 Oct`) and `formatDay` (`3 Oct`) in `src/lib/format-date.ts`; six copies of the same expression in five views and a chart preview are gone, and four sentences that wrote the day in two different ways agree.
- **A project created in another timezone passes its own gate.** A shipped test pinned the template's timezone; it states the project's own now, so `create --timezone Europe/London` no longer fails the gate it ships with.
- **A credit reads as a credit.** `formatCost` wrote `$-0.0050` for an amount under a cent; the sign goes before the currency, as `-$0.0050`.
- **Home's focus line states no ratio against a median of zero**, where it printed `NaN×`.
- **A required control inside a `Field` is announced as required.** A `Select` given `required`, or inside `<Field required>`, carries `aria-required` on its trigger, where nothing announced it.
- **Three checks in `scripts/` say what they measured.** A failed performance run shows in the coverage line rather than only in the phase; the keyboard walk prints "and the same in reverse" only when it compared the reverse walk; and `audit --atlas` reads its key list from this repository rather than from the folder it was run in. The pagination card's README no longer claims a page size in the address that the table does not keep.
- **The 0.16.0 section below was corrected in place.** Revenue's own needs were stated twice and one of them wrongly; nothing a product renders changed.

## [0.16.0] · 2026-10-15

### Breaking

- **The sample's pages are renamed.** Overview is Home, Analytics is Usage at `/usage` and Operations is Services at `/services`, with new homes at `/business` (Revenue) and `/engineering` (Now) and no redirect from an old address. The feature ids and the view tools follow: `view_overview`, `view_analytics` and `view_health` are `view_home`, `view_usage` and `view_services`. The embed views are `/embed/home` and `/embed/now` (they were `/embed/overview` and `/embed/health`), and the Services tool names no embed view. The rail labels Operations tickets and Tests and verification are Tickets and Tests; their addresses stay. An update writes none of a project's pages, configuration or views, and reports the migration `sample-pages-renamed` with every file that is one of the old pages or views or still names one. A project that kept the sample's pages as they were brings the release's over: the pages and embed views, `src/views/`, `src/data/`, `src/lib/assistant/` beside `prompt.ts`, `src/app.config.ts` with its own `app` kept, `scripts/verify.config.ts` and the shipped tests, and deletes a listed file the release no longer has. A project that made them its own keeps its own names, which nothing needs to record, makes this release's other changes in them, and gets "not applicable" for the assistant walk-through's steps on Home and Now.
- **The assistant walk-through looks for Home.** `scripts/lib/sample.ts` decides whether the walk-through applies by the folder `(home)` and the file `src/views/home-context.ts`, and `scripts/assistant.ts` and `scripts/fake-llm.ts` name `view_home`. A project that kept the sample's Overview under its old folder gets "not applicable" for that suite until it renames the folder and the file.
- **The sample surfaces include Now.** `SampleSurfaces` in `scripts/lib/sample.ts` gains `now`, true where the console has an `engineering` route and `src/views/now.tsx`. The assistant walk-through watches an approved change's Activity line on Now's "Changes" there, and says not applicable for that step without it. A script or a test of yours that builds a `SampleSurfaces` adds `now`.
- **`daily` is `points`** on the Featured metric and the Metric tile: a point of the page's Meridian need not be a day. Rename the prop where a page passes it.
- **A Metric tile's `finding` names its `tone`.** `finding` is `{ text, day?, tone }` and `tone` has no default: add `tone: 'warning'` to keep a finding as it was drawn, or `tone: 'neutral'` for a fact, which stands beside a neutral dot.
- **A tile, the Featured metric and a Sparkline read the page's cursor only when their points are the page's.** Each reads the pointed point, and a Sparkline draws its hairline and dot, only when it has one value for each of the Meridian's dates, the rule a chart joins by. Before, each read whenever the cursor fell inside its list, so a tile of fourteen days on a page of thirty read the wrong day. One that should follow the page needs a series of the page's length.
- **The Featured metric is a container.** Its section carries `@container`, so its layout answers to its own width, and it no longer sizes itself from what it holds. In a `Row` nothing changes. A card placed in a flex row with no width of its own collapses: give it `w-full`.
- **The sample's collections changed shape.** What customers pay is `revenue`: a day (`DailyPoint`), a period's totals (`Totals`) and a customer hold `revenue` where they held `spend`, in the `days` and `customers` collections and in everything that reads them: `customerFigures(rows).revenue`, `HOME_METRICS.revenue`, `CUSTOMER_METRICS.revenue`, `RevenueData` and Main's business summary. The tiles, the Customers column, the contexts and the view tools say Revenue ("Revenue, last 30 days"), defined as what customers were billed for metered usage, before credits. No figure changed. A customer's `since` is `joined`: `CustomerRecord` and the `customers` collection hold `joined`; a person still reads "Customer since". The Customers table's column key follows, so an address that sorts by it says `sort=joined`. And the sample is one company, and its figures are about ten thousand times larger: a day is some twenty to forty-five million requests and the last 30 days are $3,300,438.74 of revenue, where a day was seventy thousand requests and a month under three hundred dollars. The sample now holds 329 customers where it held twelve; the twelve keep their names and ids. A project reads its own data and is not affected unless it read the sample's: rename the field wherever a page, a test or your own data reads it (nothing answers to the old name), and a test, a page or a guide of yours that pins a figure of the sample pins a new one.
- **Customers sorts by `revenue`.** The list's default sort is `revenue` and an address that names it says `sort=revenue` (it was `sort=spend`); the bell's alert for a customer past due, Home's Business region and the customers view tool build that address. An address that still says `sort=spend` or `sort=since` is not kept working: it is handled as any unknown sort is.
- **The sample's generated records moved to `src/system/fixtures/company.ts`.** `demoSeries`, `demoTotals`, `demoHeatmap`, `ENDPOINTS` and `STATUS_MIX` are exported from there and no longer from `fixtures/sample.ts`. `CUSTOMERS` is there too: it replaces `CUSTOMER_ROWS` of `sample.ts` and `CUSTOMERS` of `sample-records.ts`. `REGIONS` there is each region's requests and `p50`, and replaces both `REGIONS` of `sample.ts` and `REGION_TRAFFIC` of `sample-ops.ts`; `WEEKDAY_HOURS` moved there from `sample-ops.ts`. The type `CustomerRecord` is exported from `fixtures/sample.ts`. Change the import path in a preview or a test that draws the sample.
- **`src/data/sample.ts` exports `REGION_NAMES`, and neither `REGIONS` nor `demoSeries`.** The request filter's list is the four region names, `REGION_NAMES`. The sign-in page reads its 30 days from `signInDays()` in `src/data/sign-in.ts`. A client view imports `src/data/sample.ts`, so it takes nothing from the module that generates the records.
- **A customer can have left.** `status` is `active`, `trial`, `past due` or `left`. A customer that has left keeps its record and is counted in nothing: `customerFigures(rows)` leaves it out of `customers`, `revenue` and `pastDue`, and so do Main's business summary, the bell and every context. The Customers list shows such a customer under the status Left only, which the filter offers, with a neutral badge. A status map of yours keyed by a customer's status needs the fourth key.
- **Every date of a customer is a day.** `joined` is `YYYY-MM-DD`, where it was an instant; `planSince`, `left`, `trialEnds` and `pastDueSince` are the same form.
- **The Customers list is handed a row, not a record.** `CustomersView` takes `CustomerRow[]` (id, name, plan, status, requests, trend, error rate, revenue, joined): pass `rows.map(customerRow)`. `customersContext` takes the same rows, and names five customers on a line where it named 25.
- **The bell reads one alert for the customers past due.** Its id is `customers:past-due` and it says "10 customers are past due", or the customer's name when there is one; there is no alert a customer any more.
- **A day holds more than a file's row.** `DailyPoint` and the `days` collection gain `tokens` and `revenueByPlan`, an object, which `ExportButton` does not take: pass `dayRows(series)` from `src/views/home-context.ts`, which writes the three plans as columns.
- **`endpoints`, `regions`, `responses` and `hours` hold the last 30 complete days.** Each adds up to the requests of those days, and an endpoint's error rate is worked out from them, so the figures moved; their fields did not.
- **The Customers view tool counts every customer.** `view_customers` reads them all, where it read the first hundred, and its `status` takes `left`; `all` is every customer that has not left.
- **`readDays(period)` returns a period of complete days, and the day in progress on its own.** `series` is the period's complete days, oldest first and ending yesterday; `previous` is the same number of complete days before them; `totals` is `{ current, previous }`, and `totals.previous` is `null` when no day comes before; `running` is the day in progress, or `null` when the latest row kept is a complete day. `HomeData` and `UsageData` follow: `totals.previous` may be `null`, and `HomeData` holds `running`. Guard `totals.previous` wherever a page, a test or a context of yours reads it.
- **`all` is every complete day kept, with nothing before it.** `ALL_DAYS` is gone and no constant stands for `all`: `readDays('all')` reads every row of `days` through `readAll()`, 360 days in the sample where it read 180 with 180 before. A figure of `all` has no change: a Metric tile says "No earlier period to compare", the Featured metric draws no change pill, and a context says no previous period is kept. `demoSeries('all')` is every complete day with an empty `previous`, `demoTotals('all').previous` is `null`, and `demoSeries` also returns `running`.
- **The day in progress is the last row of `days`, `usage`, `compute` and `costs`.** Each of the four daily collections holds the clock's own day after its complete days, with what has arrived by the clock, and its description says so: `days` has 361 rows in the sample and `usage` and `compute` 1,444. A sum of yours over one of them that should cover complete days leaves out the rows of the clock's date; `readDays` already does. The fixture arrays `DAYS`, `USAGE`, `COMPUTE` and `COSTS` still hold complete days only, and the day in progress is `IN_PROGRESS` in `src/system/fixtures/company.ts`.
- **A request holds the time to its first token.** `RequestRow` and the `requests` collection gain `ttft`, the milliseconds from arrival to the first token: a number no greater than `latency` where a model that writes tokens answered, `null` where the request reached no model, reached the embedding model, or was refused. A fixture, an action or a test of yours that builds a `RequestRow` adds it. The log is handed `LoggedRequest` rows (`src/data/requests.ts`), the record without it, and `RequestsView`, `requestsContext` and `requestColumns` take those; a request's own page reads the record whole.
- **A ticket holds when it was closed and the incident it tracks.** `Ticket` and the `tickets` collection gain `closedAt` and `incidentId`, each `null` when there is none. Rows of your own typed `Ticket` add both.
